# Abraham Lincoln

President of the United States from 1861 to 1865

For other uses, see [Abraham Lincoln (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln_\(disambiguation\) "Abraham Lincoln (disambiguation)").

"President Lincoln" redirects here. For the troopship, see [USS *President Lincoln*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_President_Lincoln "USS President Lincoln").

|                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

**Abraham Lincoln** ([/ˈlɪŋkən/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*LINK-ən*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); February 12, 1809 – April 15, 1865) was the 16th [president of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_the_United_States "President of the United States"), serving from 1861 until [his assassination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Abraham_Lincoln "Assassination of Abraham Lincoln") in 1865. He led the United States through the [American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War "American Civil War"), defending the nation as a constitutional [union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_\(American_Civil_War\) "Union (American Civil War)"), defeating the [Confederacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_States_of_America "Confederate States of America"), playing a major role in the [abolition of](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/End_of_slavery_in_the_United_States "End of slavery in the United States") [slavery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_in_the_United_States "Slavery in the United States"), expanding the power of the [federal government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_government_of_the_United_States "Federal government of the United States"), and modernizing the [U.S. economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._economy "U.S. economy").

Lincoln was born into [poverty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty "Poverty") in a [log cabin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Log_cabin "Log cabin") in [Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kentucky "Kentucky"), and was raised on the [frontier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_frontier "American frontier"), mainly in [Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana "Indiana"). He was self-educated and became a lawyer, [Whig Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whig_Party_\(United_States\) "Whig Party (United States)") leader, [Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois "Illinois") state [legislator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_House_of_Representatives "Illinois House of Representatives"), and [U.S. representative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._representative "U.S. representative") [from Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_representatives_from_Illinois "List of United States representatives from Illinois"). In 1849, he returned to his successful law practice in [Springfield, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Illinois "Springfield, Illinois"). In 1854, angered by the [Kansas–Nebraska Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas%E2%80%93Nebraska_Act "Kansas–Nebraska Act"), which opened the territories to slavery, he re-entered politics. He soon became a leader of the new [Republican Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Republican_Party_\(United_States\) "History of the Republican Party (United States)"). He reached a national audience in the [1858 Senate campaign debates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln%E2%80%93Douglas_debates "Lincoln–Douglas debates") against [Stephen A. Douglas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_A._Douglas "Stephen A. Douglas"). Lincoln ran for [president in 1860](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1860_United_States_presidential_election "1860 United States presidential election"), sweeping the [North](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_United_States "Northern United States") to gain victory. Pro-slavery elements in the [South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_United_States "Southern United States") viewed his election as a threat to slavery, and Southern states began [seceding from the nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secession_in_the_United_States "Secession in the United States"). They formed the Confederate States of America, which began seizing federal military bases in the South. A little over one month after Lincoln assumed the presidency, Confederate forces [attacked Fort Sumter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Fort_Sumter "Battle of Fort Sumter"), a U.S. fort in [South Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Carolina "South Carolina"). Following the bombardment, Lincoln mobilized forces to suppress the rebellion and restore the union.

Lincoln, a [moderate Republican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moderate_Republicans_\(Reconstruction_era\) "Moderate Republicans (Reconstruction era)"), had to navigate a contentious array of factions with friends and opponents from both the [Democratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_\(United_States\) "Democratic Party (United States)") and Republican parties. His allies, the [War Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_Democrat "War Democrat") and the [Radical Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radical_Republicans "Radical Republicans"), demanded harsh treatment of the Southern Confederates. He managed the factions by exploiting their mutual enmity, carefully distributing political patronage, and by appealing to the American people. Anti-war Democrats (called "[Copperheads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copperhead_\(politics\) "Copperhead (politics)")") despised Lincoln, and some irreconcilable pro-Confederate elements went so far as to plot his assassination. His [Gettysburg Address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gettysburg_Address "Gettysburg Address") became one of the most famous speeches in American history. Lincoln closely supervised the strategy and tactics in the war effort, including the selection of generals, and implemented a [naval blockade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_blockade "Union blockade") of the South's trade. He suspended *[habeas corpus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habeas_corpus "Habeas corpus")* in [Maryland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ex_parte_Merryman "Ex parte Merryman") and [elsewhere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habeas_Corpus_Suspension_Act_\(1863\) "Habeas Corpus Suspension Act (1863)"), and he averted war with Britain by defusing the [*Trent* Affair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trent_Affair "Trent Affair"). In 1863, he issued the [Emancipation Proclamation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emancipation_Proclamation "Emancipation Proclamation"), which declared the slaves in the states "in rebellion" to be free. It also directed the Army and Navy to "recognize and maintain the freedom of said persons" and to receive them "into the armed service of the United States." Lincoln pressured [border states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Border_states_\(American_Civil_War\) "Border states (American Civil War)") to outlaw slavery, and he promoted the [Thirteenth Amendment to the U.S. Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirteenth_Amendment_to_the_U.S._Constitution "Thirteenth Amendment to the U.S. Constitution"), which abolished slavery, except as punishment for a crime. Lincoln managed his own successful [re-election campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1864_United_States_presidential_election "1864 United States presidential election"). He sought to heal the war-torn nation through reconciliation. On April 14, 1865, just five days after the [Confederate surrender at Appomattox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Appomattox_Court_House "Battle of Appomattox Court House"), he was attending a play at [Ford's Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ford%27s_Theatre "Ford's Theatre") in Washington, D.C., with his wife, [Mary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Todd_Lincoln "Mary Todd Lincoln"), when he was fatally shot by Confederate sympathizer [John Wilkes Booth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Wilkes_Booth "John Wilkes Booth").

Lincoln is remembered as a [martyr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyr "Martyr") and a national hero for his wartime leadership and for his efforts to preserve the Union and abolish slavery. He is often [ranked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_rankings_of_presidents_of_the_United_States#Scholar_survey_summary "Historical rankings of presidents of the United States") in both popular and scholarly polls as the greatest president in American history.

## Family and childhood

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=1 "Edit section: Family and childhood")]

### Early life

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=2 "Edit section: Early life")]

Main article: [Early life and career of Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_life_and_career_of_Abraham_Lincoln "Early life and career of Abraham Lincoln")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Log_Cabin_at_the_Lincoln_Living_Historical_Farm.jpg/220px-Log_Cabin_at_the_Lincoln_Living_Historical_Farm.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Log_Cabin_at_the_Lincoln_Living_Historical_Farm.jpg)

The farm site in [Spencer County, Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spencer_County,_Indiana "Spencer County, Indiana"), where Lincoln was born and lived the first two years of his life[\[2\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-3)

Lincoln was born on February 12, 1809, the second child of [Thomas Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Lincoln "Thomas Lincoln") and [Nancy Hanks Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Lincoln "Nancy Lincoln"), in a log cabin on [Sinking Spring Farm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln_Birthplace_National_Historical_Park "Abraham Lincoln Birthplace National Historical Park") near [Hodgenville, Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hodgenville,_Kentucky "Hodgenville, Kentucky").[\[3\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199620%E2%80%9322-4) He was a descendant of [Samuel Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samuel_Lincoln "Samuel Lincoln"), an Englishman who migrated from [Hingham, Norfolk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hingham,_Norfolk "Hingham, Norfolk"), to its namesake, [Hingham, Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hingham,_Massachusetts "Hingham, Massachusetts"), in 1638. The family through subsequent generations migrated west, passing through [New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey "New Jersey"), [Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania "Pennsylvania"), and [Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia "Virginia").[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWarren20173%E2%80%934-5) Lincoln was also a descendant of the [Harrison family of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrison_family_of_Virginia "Harrison family of Virginia"); his paternal grandfather and namesake, [Captain Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln_\(captain\) "Abraham Lincoln (captain)") and wife Bathsheba (née Herring) moved the family from Virginia to [Jefferson County, Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jefferson_County,_Kentucky "Jefferson County, Kentucky").[\[b\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-7) The captain was killed in an [Indian raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Indian_War "Northwest Indian War") in 1786.[\[6\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWarren20174-8) His children, including eight-year-old Thomas, Abraham's father, witnessed the attack.[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199621-9)[\[c\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-11) Thomas then worked at odd jobs in Kentucky and [Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee "Tennessee") before the family settled in [Hardin County, Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hardin_County,_Kentucky "Hardin County, Kentucky"), in the early 1800s.[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199621-9)

Lincoln's mother [Nancy Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Lincoln "Nancy Lincoln") is widely assumed to be the daughter of Lucy Hanks.[\[9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBartelt200879-12) Thomas and Nancy married on June 12, 1806, in Washington County, and moved to [Elizabethtown, Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabethtown,_Kentucky "Elizabethtown, Kentucky").[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWarren20179-13) They had three children: [Sarah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Lincoln_Grigsby "Sarah Lincoln Grigsby"), Abraham, and Thomas, who died as an infant.[\[11\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWarren20179%E2%80%9310-14)

Thomas Lincoln bought multiple farms in Kentucky, but could not get clear [property titles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recording_\(real_estate\) "Recording (real estate)") to any, losing hundreds of acres of land in property disputes.[\[12\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199622%E2%80%9324-15) In 1816, the family moved to [Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana "Indiana"), where the land surveys and titles were more reliable.[\[13\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWarren201713-16) They settled in an "unbroken forest"[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWarren201726-17) in [Little Pigeon Creek Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Pigeon_Creek_Community "Little Pigeon Creek Community"), Hurricane Township, [Perry County, Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Perry_County,_Indiana "History of Perry County, Indiana").[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWarren201716,_43-18) When the Lincolns moved to Indiana it had just been [admitted to the Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admission_to_the_Union "Admission to the Union") as a ["free" (non-slaveholding)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slave_states_and_free_states "Slave states and free states") state,[\[16\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-19) except that, though "no new enslaved people were allowed, ... currently enslaved individuals remained so".[\[17\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-20)[\[d\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-22) In 1860, Lincoln noted that the family's move to Indiana was "partly on account of slavery", but mainly due to land title difficulties.[\[19\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199623%E2%80%9324-23)[\[20\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-24) In Kentucky and Indiana, Thomas worked as a farmer, cabinetmaker, and carpenter.[\[21\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBartelt200834,_156-25) At various times he owned farms, livestock, and town lots, paid taxes, sat on juries, appraised estates, and served on county patrols. Thomas and Nancy were members of a [Separate Baptist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separate_Baptists "Separate Baptists"), which "condemned profanity, intoxication, gossip, horse racing, and dancing." Most of its members opposed slavery.[\[22\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199624-26)

Overcoming financial challenges, Thomas in 1827 obtained [clear title](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clear_title "Clear title") to 80 acres (32 ha) in Indiana, an area that became known as [Little Pigeon Creek Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Pigeon_Creek_Community "Little Pigeon Creek Community").[\[23\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBartelt200824,_104-27)

### Mother's death

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=3 "Edit section: Mother's death")]

On October 5, 1818, Nancy Lincoln died from [milk sickness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milk_sickness "Milk sickness"), leaving 11-year-old Sarah in charge of a household including her father, nine-year-old Abraham, and Nancy's 19-year-old orphan cousin, Dennis Hanks.[\[24\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBartelt200822%E2%80%9323,_77-28) Ten years later, on January 20, 1828, Sarah died while giving birth to a stillborn son, devastating Lincoln.[\[25\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199634,_116-29)

On December 2, 1819, Thomas married [Sarah Bush Johnston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Bush_Lincoln "Sarah Bush Lincoln"), a widow from Elizabethtown, Kentucky, with three children of her own.[\[26\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBartelt200823,_83-30) Abraham became close to his stepmother and called her "Mother".[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199626%E2%80%9327-31) Dennis Hanks said he was lazy, for all his "reading—scribbling—writing—ciphering—writing poetry".[\[28\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-32) His stepmother acknowledged he did not enjoy "physical labor" but loved to read.[\[29\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBartelt200866-33)[\[30\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite200930-34)

### Education and move to Illinois

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=4 "Edit section: Education and move to Illinois")]

Lincoln was largely self-educated.[\[31\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBartelt200810,_33-35) His formal schooling was from [itinerant teachers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Itinerant_teacher "Itinerant teacher"). It included two short stints in Kentucky, where he learned to read, but probably not to write. In Indiana at age seven,[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199623-36) due to farm chores, he attended school only sporadically, for a total of fewer than 12 months in aggregate by age 15.[\[33\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199629-37) Nonetheless, he remained an avid reader and retained a lifelong interest in learning.[\[34\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMadison2014110-38) Family, neighbors, and schoolmates recalled that his readings included the [King James Bible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_James_Version "King James Version"), [Aesop's Fables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aesop%27s_Fables "Aesop's Fables"), [John Bunyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Bunyan "John Bunyan")'s *[The Pilgrim's Progress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pilgrim%27s_Progress "The Pilgrim's Progress")*, [Daniel Defoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Defoe "Daniel Defoe")'s *[Robinson Crusoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robinson_Crusoe "Robinson Crusoe")*, and *[The Autobiography of Benjamin Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Autobiography_of_Benjamin_Franklin "The Autobiography of Benjamin Franklin")*.[\[35\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199629%E2%80%9331,_38%E2%80%9343-39) Despite being self-educated, Lincoln was the recipient of [honorary degrees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honorary_degree "Honorary degree") later in life, including an honorary [Doctor of Laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctor_of_Law "Doctor of Law") from [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University "Columbia University") in June 1861.[\[36\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-40)

When Lincoln was a teen, his "father grew more and more to depend on him for the 'farming, grubbing, hoeing, making fences' necessary to keep the family afloat. He also regularly hired his son out to work ... and by law, he was entitled to everything the boy earned until he came of age".[\[37\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199632-41) Lincoln was tall, strong, and athletic, and became adept at using an ax.[\[38\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWarren2017134%E2%80%93135-42) He was an active wrestler during his youth and trained in the rough [catch-as-catch-can](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catch_wrestling "Catch wrestling") style (also known as catch wrestling). He became county wrestling champion at the age of 21.[\[39\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-43) He gained a reputation for his strength and audacity after winning a wrestling match with the renowned leader of ruffians known as the Clary's Grove boys.[\[40\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199640%E2%80%9341-44)

In March 1830, fearing another milk sickness outbreak, several members of the extended Lincoln family, including Abraham, moved west to Illinois, a free state, and settled in [Macon County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macon_County,_Illinois "Macon County, Illinois").[\[41\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199636-45)[\[e\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-47) Abraham then became increasingly distant from Thomas, in part, due to his father's lack of interest in education.[\[43\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBartelt200871-48) In 1831, as Thomas and other family members prepared to move to a [new homestead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Log_Cabin_State_Historic_Site "Lincoln Log Cabin State Historic Site") in [Coles County, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coles_County,_Illinois "Coles County, Illinois"), Abraham struck out on his own.[\[44\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOates197415%E2%80%9317-49) He made his home in [New Salem, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln%27s_New_Salem "Lincoln's New Salem"), for six years.[\[45\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThomas200823%E2%80%9353-50) Lincoln and some friends took goods, including live hogs, by [flatboat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flatboat "Flatboat") to [New Orleans, Louisiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans,_Louisiana "New Orleans, Louisiana"), where he first witnessed slavery.[\[46\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTESandburg1926202Donald199638-51)

### Marriage and children

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=5 "Edit section: Marriage and children")]

Further information: [Lincoln family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_family "Lincoln family"), [Health of Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_of_Abraham_Lincoln "Health of Abraham Lincoln"), and [Sexuality of Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality_of_Abraham_Lincoln "Sexuality of Abraham Lincoln")

[![A seated Lincoln holding a book as his young son looks at it](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/A%26TLincoln.jpg/192px-A%26TLincoln.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:A%26TLincoln.jpg)

President Lincoln with his youngest son, [Tad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tad_Lincoln "Tad Lincoln"), in 1864

[![Black and white photo of Mary Todd Lincoln's shoulders and head](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/Mary_Todd_Lincoln2crop.jpg/164px-Mary_Todd_Lincoln2crop.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Mary_Todd_Lincoln2crop.jpg)

[Mary Todd Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Todd_Lincoln "Mary Todd Lincoln"), Lincoln's wife, c. 1861

Speculation persists that Lincoln's first romantic interest was [Ann Rutledge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ann_Rutledge "Ann Rutledge"), whom he met when he moved to New Salem. However, witness testimony, given decades afterward, showed a lack of any specific recollection of a romance between the two.[\[47\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-52) Rutledge died on August 25, 1835, most likely of [typhoid fever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhoid_fever "Typhoid fever"); Lincoln took the death very hard, saying that he could not bear the idea of rain falling on Ann's grave. Lincoln sank into a serious episode of depression, and this gave rise to speculation that he had been in love with her.[\[48\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199655%E2%80%9358-53)[\[49\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Atlanticoct2005-54)[\[50\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-55)

In the early 1830s, he met [Mary Owens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Owens_\(Abraham_Lincoln_fianc%C3%A9e\) "Mary Owens (Abraham Lincoln fiancée)") from Kentucky.[\[51\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThomas200856%E2%80%9357,_69%E2%80%9370-56) Late in 1836, Lincoln agreed to a match with Owens if she returned to New Salem. Owens arrived that November and he courted her; however, they both had second thoughts. On August 16, 1837, he wrote Owens a letter saying he would not blame her if she ended the relationship, and she never replied.[\[52\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199667-57)

In 1839, Lincoln met [Mary Todd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Todd_Lincoln "Mary Todd Lincoln") in [Springfield, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Illinois "Springfield, Illinois"), and the following year they became engaged.[\[53\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199680%E2%80%9386-58) She was the daughter of [Robert Smith Todd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Smith_Todd "Robert Smith Todd"), a wealthy lawyer and businessman in [Lexington, Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lexington,_Kentucky "Lexington, Kentucky").[\[54\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELambSwain20083-59) Their wedding, which was set for January 1, 1841, was canceled because Lincoln did not appear, but they reconciled and married on November 4, 1842, in the Springfield home of Mary's sister.[\[55\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTESandburg1926260,_290%E2%80%93291-60) While anxiously preparing for the nuptials, he was asked where he was going and replied, "To hell, I suppose".[\[56\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199693-61) In 1844, the couple bought [a house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Home_National_Historic_Site "Lincoln Home National Historic Site") in Springfield near his law office. Mary kept house with the help of a hired servant and a relative.[\[57\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBaker1989142-62)

Lincoln was an affectionate husband and father of four sons, though his work regularly kept him away from home. The eldest, [Robert Todd Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Todd_Lincoln "Robert Todd Lincoln"), was born in 1843, and was the only child to live to maturity. [Edward Baker Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Baker_Lincoln "Edward Baker Lincoln") (Eddie), born in 1846, died February 1, 1850, probably of tuberculosis. Lincoln's third son, ["Willie" Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Wallace_Lincoln "William Wallace Lincoln"), was born on December 21, 1850, and died of a fever at the [White House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House "White House") on February 20, 1862. The youngest, [Thomas "Tad" Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tad_Lincoln "Tad Lincoln"), was born on April 4, 1853, and survived his father, but died of heart failure at age 18 on July 16, 1871.[\[58\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009179%E2%80%93181,_476-63)[\[f\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-65)

Lincoln "was remarkably fond of children"[\[60\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009126-66) and the Lincolns were not considered to be strict with their own.[\[61\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBaker1989120-67) In fact, Lincoln's law partner [William H. Herndon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_H._Herndon "William H. Herndon") would grow irritated when Lincoln brought his children to the law office. Their father, it seemed, was often too absorbed in his work to notice his children's behavior. Herndon recounted, "I have felt many and many a time that I wanted to wring their little necks, and yet out of respect for Lincoln I kept my mouth shut. Lincoln did not note what his children were doing or had done."[\[62\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-68)

The deaths of their sons Eddie and Willie had profound effects on both parents. Lincoln suffered from "[melancholy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_depression "History of depression")", a condition now thought to be [clinical depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_depressive_disorder "Major depressive disorder").[\[49\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Atlanticoct2005-54) Later in life, Mary struggled with the stresses of losing her husband and sons, and in 1875 Robert committed her to an asylum.[\[63\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTESteers2010341-69)

## Early career and militia service

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=6 "Edit section: Early career and militia service")]

Further information: [Early life and career of Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_life_and_career_of_Abraham_Lincoln "Early life and career of Abraham Lincoln") and [Abraham Lincoln in the Black Hawk War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln_in_the_Black_Hawk_War "Abraham Lincoln in the Black Hawk War")

During 1831 and 1832, Lincoln worked at a general store in [New Salem, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln%27s_New_Salem "Lincoln's New Salem"). In 1832, he declared his candidacy for the [Illinois House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_House_of_Representatives "Illinois House of Representatives"), but interrupted his campaign to serve as a captain in the [Illinois Militia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Militia "Illinois Militia") during the [Black Hawk War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Hawk_War "Black Hawk War").[\[64\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWinkle200186%E2%80%9395-70) When Lincoln returned home from the [Black Hawk War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Hawk_War "Black Hawk War"), he planned to become a blacksmith, but instead formed a partnership with 21-year-old William Berry, with whom he purchased a New Salem general store on credit. Because a license was required to sell customers beverages, Berry obtained bartending licenses for $7 each for Lincoln and himself, and in 1833 the [Lincoln-Berry General Store](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln-Berry_General_Store "Lincoln-Berry General Store") became a tavern as well.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

As licensed bartenders, Lincoln and Berry were able to sell spirits, including liquor, for 12 cents a pint. They offered a wide range of alcoholic beverages as well as food, including takeout dinners. But Berry became an alcoholic, was often too drunk to work, and Lincoln ended up running the store by himself.[\[65\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-71) Although the economy was booming, the business struggled and went into debt, causing Lincoln to sell his share.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

In his first campaign speech after returning from his military service, Lincoln observed a supporter in the crowd under attack, grabbed the assailant by his "neck and the seat of his trousers", and tossed him.[\[41\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199636-45) In the campaign, Lincoln advocated for navigational improvements on the [Sangamon River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangamon_River "Sangamon River"). He could draw crowds as a [raconteur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raconteur "Raconteur"), but lacked the requisite formal education, powerful friends, and money, and lost the election.[\[66\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-72) Lincoln finished eighth out of 13 candidates (the top four were elected), though he received 277 of the 300 votes cast in the New Salem precinct.[\[67\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWinkle2001114%E2%80%93116-73)

Lincoln served as New Salem's postmaster and later as county surveyor, but continued his voracious reading and decided to become a lawyer.[\[68\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Zofia-74) Rather than studying in the office of an established attorney, as was the custom, Lincoln borrowed legal texts from attorneys [John Todd Stuart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Todd_Stuart "John Todd Stuart") and [Thomas Drummond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Drummond_\(judge\) "Thomas Drummond (judge)"), purchased books including [Blackstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Blackstone "William Blackstone")'s *[Commentaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commentaries_on_the_Laws_of_England "Commentaries on the Laws of England")* and [Chitty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Chitty "Joseph Chitty")'s *Pleadings*, and [read law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reading_law "Reading law") on his own.[\[68\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Zofia-74) He later said of his legal education that "I studied with nobody."[\[69\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199653%E2%80%9355-75)

## Illinois state legislature (1834–1842)

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=7 "Edit section: Illinois state legislature (1834–1842)")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Abes_House.JPG/220px-Abes_House.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Abes_House.JPG)

Lincoln's [home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Home_National_Historic_Site "Lincoln Home National Historic Site") in [Springfield, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Illinois "Springfield, Illinois"), where he resided from 1844 until becoming the nation's 16th [president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_the_United_States "President of the United States") in 1861

Lincoln's second state house campaign in 1834, this time as a [Whig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whig_Party_\(United_States\) "Whig Party (United States)"), was a success over a powerful Whig opponent.[\[70\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite200959-76) Then followed his four terms in the [Illinois House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_House_of_Representatives "Illinois House of Representatives") for [Sangamon County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangamon_County "Sangamon County").[\[71\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTESimon1990283-77) He championed construction of the [Illinois and Michigan Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_and_Michigan_Canal "Illinois and Michigan Canal"), and later was a Canal Commissioner.[\[72\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-78) He voted to expand suffrage beyond white landowners to all white males, but adopted a "free soil" stance opposing both slavery and [abolition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abolitionism_in_the_United_States "Abolitionism in the United States").[\[73\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTESimon1990130-79) In 1837, he declared, "\[The] Institution of slavery is founded on both injustice and bad policy, but the promulgation of abolition doctrines tends rather to increase than abate its evils."[\[74\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996134-80) He echoed [Henry Clay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Clay "Henry Clay")'s support for the [American Colonization Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Colonization_Society "American Colonization Society") which advocated a program of abolition in conjunction with settling freed slaves in [Liberia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberia "Liberia").[\[75\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFoner201017%E2%80%9319,_67-81)

He was [admitted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admission_to_the_bar_in_the_United_States "Admission to the bar in the United States") to the Illinois bar on September 9, 1836,[\[76\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199664-82)[\[77\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-83) and moved to Springfield and began to practice law under [John T. Stuart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_T._Stuart "John T. Stuart"), Mary Todd's cousin.[\[78\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite200971,_79,_108-84) Lincoln emerged as a formidable trial combatant during cross-examinations and closing arguments. He partnered several years with [Stephen T. Logan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_T._Logan "Stephen T. Logan"), and in 1844, began [his practice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln-Herndon_Law_Offices_State_Historic_Site "Lincoln-Herndon Law Offices State Historic Site") with [William Herndon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Herndon_\(lawyer\) "William Herndon (lawyer)"), "a studious young man".[\[79\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199617-85)

On January 27, 1838, Abraham Lincoln, then 28 years old, delivered his [first major speech at the Lyceum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln%27s_Lyceum_address "Abraham Lincoln's Lyceum address") in [Springfield, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Illinois "Springfield, Illinois"), after the murder of newspaper editor [Elijah Parish Lovejoy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elijah_Parish_Lovejoy "Elijah Parish Lovejoy") in Alton. Lincoln warned that no trans-Atlantic military giant could ever crush the U.S. as a nation. "It cannot come from abroad. If destruction be our lot, we must ourselves be its author and finisher", said Lincoln.[\[80\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-86)[\[81\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-87) Prior to that, on April 28, 1836, a black man, [Francis McIntosh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lynching_of_Francis_McIntosh "Lynching of Francis McIntosh"), was burned alive in [St. Louis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Louis "St. Louis"), [Missouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missouri "Missouri"). Zann Gill describes how these two murders set off a chain reaction that ultimately prompted Abraham Lincoln to run for President.[\[82\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-88)

## U.S. House of Representatives (1847–1849)

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=8 "Edit section: U.S. House of Representatives (1847–1849)")]

[![Middle-aged clean-shaven Lincoln from the hips up.](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Abraham_Lincoln_by_Nicholas_Shepherd%2C_1846-crop.jpg/170px-Abraham_Lincoln_by_Nicholas_Shepherd%2C_1846-crop.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Abraham_Lincoln_by_Nicholas_Shepherd,_1846-crop.jpg)

Lincoln in his late 30s as a member of the [U.S. House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_House_of_Representatives "United States House of Representatives"), c. 1846

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/1846_Illinois_US_House_District_7_results.svg/150px-1846_Illinois_US_House_District_7_results.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:1846_Illinois_US_House_District_7_results.svg)

1846 Illinois U.S. House District 7 results by county\


  Lincoln

-      30%-40%      50%-60%      60%-70%      70%-80%

  Cartwright

-      50%-60%

True to his record, Lincoln professed to friends in 1861 to be "an old line Whig, a disciple of Henry Clay".[\[83\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996222-89) Their party favored economic modernization in banking, tariffs to fund [internal improvements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_improvements "Internal improvements") including railroads, and urbanization.[\[84\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBorittPinsker2002137%E2%80%93153-90)

In 1843, Lincoln sought the Whig nomination for [Illinois's 7th district seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois%27s_7th_congressional_district "Illinois's 7th congressional district") in the [U.S. House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_House_of_Representatives "United States House of Representatives"); he was defeated by [John J. Hardin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_J._Hardin "John J. Hardin"), though he prevailed with the party in limiting Hardin to one term. Lincoln not only pulled off his strategy of gaining the nomination in 1846, but also won the election. He was the only Whig in the Illinois delegation, but as dutiful as any participated in almost all votes and made speeches that toed the party line.[\[85\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOates197479-91) He was assigned to the [Committee on Post Office and Post Roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_House_Committee_on_Post_Office_and_Post_Roads "United States House Committee on Post Office and Post Roads") and the [Committee on Expenditures in the War Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_House_Committee_on_Expenditures_in_the_War_Department "United States House Committee on Expenditures in the War Department").[\[86\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-92) Lincoln teamed with [Joshua R. Giddings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joshua_R._Giddings "Joshua R. Giddings") on a bill to abolish slavery in the [District of Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington,_District_of_Columbia "Washington, District of Columbia") with compensation for the owners, enforcement to capture fugitive slaves, and a popular vote on the matter. He dropped the bill when it eluded Whig support.[\[87\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarris200754Foner201057-93)[\[88\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-94)

### Political views

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=9 "Edit section: Political views")]

On foreign and military policy, Lincoln spoke against the [Mexican–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican%E2%80%93American_War "Mexican–American War"), which he imputed President [James K. Polk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_K._Polk "James K. Polk")'s desire for "military glory — that attractive rainbow, that rises in showers of blood".[\[89\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeidlerHeidler2006181%E2%80%93183-95) He supported the [Wilmot Proviso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilmot_Proviso "Wilmot Proviso"), a failed proposal to ban slavery in any U.S. territory won from Mexico.[\[90\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolzer200463-96)

Lincoln emphasized his opposition to Polk by drafting and introducing his [Spot Resolutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spot_Resolutions "Spot Resolutions"). The war had begun with a killing of American soldiers by Mexican cavalry patrol in disputed territory, and Polk insisted that Mexican soldiers had "invaded our territory and shed the blood of our fellow-citizens on our own soil".[\[91\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOates197479%E2%80%9380-97) Lincoln demanded that Polk show Congress the exact spot on which blood had been shed and prove that the spot was on American soil.[\[92\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraebner1959199%E2%80%93202-98) The resolution was ignored in both Congress and the national papers, and it cost Lincoln political support in his district. One Illinois newspaper derisively nicknamed him "spotty Lincoln".[\[93\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-99) Lincoln later regretted some of his statements, especially his attack on presidential war-making powers.[\[94\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996128-100)

Lincoln had pledged in 1846 to serve only one term in the House. Realizing Clay was unlikely to win the presidency, he supported General [Zachary Taylor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zachary_Taylor "Zachary Taylor") for the Whig nomination in the [1848 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1848_United_States_presidential_election "1848 United States presidential election").[\[95\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996124%E2%80%93126-101) Taylor won and Lincoln hoped in vain to be appointed Commissioner of the [United States General Land Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_General_Land_Office "United States General Land Office").[\[96\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996140-102) The administration offered to appoint him secretary or governor of the [Oregon Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oregon_Territory "Oregon Territory") as consolation.[\[97\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-103) This distant territory was a Democratic stronghold, and acceptance of the post would have disrupted his legal and political career in Illinois, so he declined and resumed his law practice.[\[98\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarris200755%E2%80%9357-104)

## Prairie lawyer

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=10 "Edit section: Prairie lawyer")]

See also: [List of cases involving Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cases_involving_Abraham_Lincoln "List of cases involving Abraham Lincoln")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/Abraham_Lincoln_by_Hesler%2C_1857.jpg/170px-Abraham_Lincoln_by_Hesler%2C_1857.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Abraham_Lincoln_by_Hesler,_1857.jpg)

Lincoln in February 1857

In his Springfield practice, Lincoln handled "every kind of business that could come before a prairie lawyer".[\[99\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald199696-105) Twice a year he appeared for 10 consecutive weeks in county seats in the Midstate county courts; this continued for 16 years.[\[100\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996105%E2%80%93106,_158-106) Lincoln handled transportation cases in the midst of the nation's western expansion, particularly river barge conflicts under the many new railroad bridges. As a riverboat man, Lincoln initially favored those interests, but ultimately represented whoever hired him.[\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996142%E2%80%93143-107) He later represented a bridge company against a riverboat company in *[Hurd v. Rock Island Bridge Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurd_v._Rock_Island_Bridge_Company "Hurd v. Rock Island Bridge Company")*, a landmark case involving a canal boat that sank after hitting a bridge.[\[102\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-108) In 1849 he received [a patent for a flotation device](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln%27s_patent "Abraham Lincoln's patent") for the movement of boats in shallow water. The idea was never commercialized, but it made Lincoln the only president to hold a patent.[\[103\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-109)

Lincoln appeared before the Illinois Supreme Court in 175 cases; he was sole counsel in 51 cases, of which 31 were decided in his favor.[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichards2015440-110) From 1853 to 1860, one of his largest clients was the [Illinois Central Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Central_Railroad "Illinois Central Railroad").[\[105\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996155%E2%80%93156,_196%E2%80%93197-111) His legal reputation gave rise to the nickname "Honest Abe".[\[106\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-112)

In an 1858 criminal trial, Lincoln represented William "Duff" Armstrong, who was on trial for the murder of James Preston Metzker.[\[107\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996150%E2%80%93151-113) The case is famous for Lincoln's use of a fact established by [judicial notice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judicial_notice "Judicial notice") to challenge the credibility of an eyewitness. After an opposing witness testified to seeing the crime in the moonlight, Lincoln produced a *[Farmers' Almanac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farmers%27_Almanac "Farmers' Almanac")* showing the Moon was at a low angle, drastically reducing visibility. Armstrong was acquitted.[\[107\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996150%E2%80%93151-113)

In an 1859 murder case, leading up to his presidential campaign, Lincoln elevated his profile with his defense of Simeon Quinn "Peachy" Harrison, who was a third cousin;[\[g\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-115) Harrison was also the grandson of Lincoln's political opponent, [Rev. Peter Cartwright](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Cartwright_\(revivalist\) "Peter Cartwright (revivalist)").[\[109\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarrison1935-116) Harrison was charged with the murder of Greek Crafton who, as he lay dying of his wounds, confessed to Cartwright that he had provoked Harrison.[\[110\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-117) Lincoln angrily protested the judge's initial decision to exclude Cartwright's testimony about the confession as inadmissible [hearsay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hearsay "Hearsay"). Lincoln argued that the testimony involved a [dying declaration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dying_declaration "Dying declaration") and was not subject to the hearsay rule. Instead of holding Lincoln in [contempt of court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contempt_of_court#United_States "Contempt of court") as expected, the judge, a Democrat, reversed his ruling and admitted the testimony into evidence, resulting in Harrison's acquittal.[\[107\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996150%E2%80%93151-113)

## Republican politics (1854–1860)

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=11 "Edit section: Republican politics (1854–1860)")]

Main article: [Abraham Lincoln in politics, 1849–1861](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln_in_politics,_1849%E2%80%931861 "Abraham Lincoln in politics, 1849–1861")

### Emergence as Republican leader

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=12 "Edit section: Emergence as Republican leader")]

Further information: [Slave states and free states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slave_states_and_free_states "Slave states and free states") and [Abraham Lincoln and slavery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln_and_slavery "Abraham Lincoln and slavery")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Abraham_Lincoln_by_Byers%2C_1858_-_crop.jpg/170px-Abraham_Lincoln_by_Byers%2C_1858_-_crop.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Abraham_Lincoln_by_Byers,_1858_-_crop.jpg)

Lincoln in May 1858, the year of [his debates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln%E2%80%93Douglas_debates "Lincoln–Douglas debates") with [Stephen Douglas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_A._Douglas "Stephen A. Douglas") over slavery

The debate over the status of slavery in the territories failed to alleviate tensions between the slave-holding South and the free North, with the failure of the [Compromise of 1850](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compromise_of_1850 "Compromise of 1850"), a legislative package designed to address the issue.[\[111\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009175%E2%80%93176-118) In his 1852 eulogy for Clay, Lincoln highlighted the latter's support for gradual emancipation and opposition to "both extremes" on the slavery issue.[\[112\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009182%E2%80%93185-119) As the slavery debate in the [Nebraska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nebraska_Territory "Nebraska Territory") and [Kansas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas_Territory "Kansas Territory") territories became particularly acrimonious, Illinois Senator Stephen A. Douglas proposed [popular sovereignty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_sovereignty_in_the_United_States#Emergence_of_the_term_%22popular_sovereignty%22_and_its_pejorative_connotation "Popular sovereignty in the United States") as a compromise; the measure would allow the electorate of each territory to decide the status of slavery. The legislation alarmed many Northerners, who sought to prevent the spread of slavery that could result, but Douglas's [Kansas–Nebraska Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas%E2%80%93Nebraska_Act "Kansas–Nebraska Act") narrowly passed Congress in May 1854.[\[113\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009188%E2%80%93190-120)

Lincoln did not comment on the act until months later in his "[Peoria Speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln%27s_Peoria_speech "Abraham Lincoln's Peoria speech")" of October 1854. Lincoln then declared his opposition to slavery, which he repeated en route to the presidency.[\[114\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThomas2008148%E2%80%93152-121) He said the Kansas Act had a "*declared* indifference, but as I must think, a covert *real* zeal for the spread of slavery. I cannot but hate it. I hate it because of the monstrous injustice of slavery itself. I hate it because it deprives our republican example of its just influence in the world...."[\[115\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraebner1959255-122) Lincoln's attacks on the Kansas–Nebraska Act marked his return to political life.[\[116\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009203%E2%80%93205-123)

Nationally, the Whigs were irreparably split by the Kansas–Nebraska Act and other efforts to compromise on the slavery issue. Reflecting on the demise of his party, Lincoln wrote in 1855, "I think I am a Whig, but others say there are no Whigs, and that I am an abolitionist. ... I do no more than oppose the *extension* of slavery."[\[117\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009215%E2%80%93216-124) The new [Republican Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Party_\(United_States\) "Republican Party (United States)") was formed as a northern party dedicated to antislavery, drawing from the antislavery wing of the Whig Party and combining [Free Soil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Soil_Party "Free Soil Party"), [Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_Party_\(United_States,_1840\) "Liberty Party (United States, 1840)"), and antislavery [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_\(United_States\) "Democratic Party (United States)") members,[\[118\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcGovern200938%E2%80%9339-125) Lincoln resisted early Republican entreaties, fearing that the new party would become a platform for extreme abolitionists.[\[119\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009203%E2%80%93204-126) Lincoln held out hope for rejuvenating the Whigs, though he lamented his party's growing closeness with the nativist [Know Nothing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Know_Nothing "Know Nothing") movement.[\[120\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009191%E2%80%93194-127)

In 1854, Lincoln was elected to the Illinois legislature, but before the term began the following January he declined to take his seat so that he would be eligible to be a candidate in the upcoming U.S. Senate election.[\[121\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-128)[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-129) The year's elections showed the strong opposition to the Kansas–Nebraska Act, and in the aftermath Lincoln sought election to the U.S. Senate.[\[116\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009203%E2%80%93205-123) At that time, senators were elected by state legislatures.[\[123\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOates1974119-130) After leading in the first six rounds of voting, he was unable to obtain a majority. Lincoln instructed his backers to vote for [Lyman Trumbull](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyman_Trumbull "Lyman Trumbull"). Trumbull was an antislavery Democrat and had received few votes in the earlier ballots; his supporters, also antislavery Democrats, had vowed not to support any Whig. Lincoln's decision to withdraw enabled his Whig supporters and Trumbull's antislavery Democrats to combine and defeat the mainstream Democratic candidate, [Joel Aldrich Matteson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joel_Aldrich_Matteson "Joel Aldrich Matteson").[\[124\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009205%E2%80%93208-131)

#### 1856 campaign

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=13 "Edit section: 1856 campaign")]

[Violent political confrontations in Kansas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bleeding_Kansas "Bleeding Kansas") continued, and opposition to the Kansas–Nebraska Act remained strong throughout the North. As the [1856 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1856_United_States_elections "1856 United States elections") approached, Lincoln joined the [Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Party_\(United_States\) "Republican Party (United States)") and attended the [Bloomington Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomington_Convention "Bloomington Convention"), where the [Illinois Republican Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Republican_Party "Illinois Republican Party") was established . The convention platform endorsed Congress's right to regulate slavery in the territories and backed the admission of Kansas as a free state. Lincoln gave the [final speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln%27s_Lost_Speech "Lincoln's Lost Speech") of the convention supporting the party platform and called for the preservation of the Union.[\[125\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009216%E2%80%93221-132) At the June [1856 Republican National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1856_Republican_National_Convention "1856 Republican National Convention"), though Lincoln received support to run as vice president, [John C. Frémont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_C._Fr%C3%A9mont "John C. Frémont") and [William Dayton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Dayton "William Dayton") were on the ticket, which Lincoln supported throughout Illinois. The Democrats nominated former Secretary of State [James Buchanan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Buchanan "James Buchanan") and the Know-Nothings nominated former Whig President [Millard Fillmore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millard_Fillmore "Millard Fillmore").[\[126\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009224%E2%80%93228-133) Buchanan prevailed, while Republican [William Henry Bissell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Henry_Bissell "William Henry Bissell") won election as Governor of Illinois, and Lincoln became a leading Republican in Illinois.[\[127\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009229%E2%80%93230-134)[\[h\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-136)

#### *Dred Scott v. Sandford*

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=14 "Edit section: Dred Scott v. Sandford")]

[Dred Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dred_Scott "Dred Scott") was a slave whose master took him from a slave state to a territory that was free as a result of the [Missouri Compromise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missouri_Compromise "Missouri Compromise"). After Scott was returned to the slave state, he petitioned a federal court for his freedom. His petition was denied in *[Dred Scott v. Sandford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dred_Scott_v._Sandford "Dred Scott v. Sandford")* (1857).[\[i\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-138) In his opinion, Supreme Court Chief Justice [Roger B. Taney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_B._Taney "Roger B. Taney") wrote that black people were not citizens and derived no rights from the Constitution, and that the Missouri Compromise was unconstitutional for infringing upon slave owners' "property" rights. While many Democrats hoped that *Dred Scott* would end the dispute over slavery in the territories, the decision sparked further outrage in the North.[\[130\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009236%E2%80%93238-139) Lincoln denounced it as the product of a conspiracy of Democrats to support the [Slave Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slave_Power "Slave Power").[\[131\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEZarefsky199369%E2%80%93110-140) He argued the decision was at variance with the Declaration of Independence; he said that while the founding fathers did not believe all men equal in every respect, they believed all men were equal "in certain inalienable rights, among which are life, liberty, and the pursuit of happiness".[\[132\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJaffa2000299%E2%80%93300-141)

### Lincoln–Douglas debates and Cooper Union speech

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=15 "Edit section: Lincoln–Douglas debates and Cooper Union speech")]

Further information: [Lincoln–Douglas debates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln%E2%80%93Douglas_debates "Lincoln–Douglas debates") and [Cooper Union speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooper_Union_speech "Cooper Union speech")

In 1858, Douglas was up for re-election in the U.S. Senate, and Lincoln hoped to defeat him. Many in the party felt that a former Whig should be nominated in 1858, and Lincoln's 1856 campaigning and support of Trumbull had earned him a favor.[\[133\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009247%E2%80%93248-142) Some eastern Republicans supported Douglas for his opposition to the [Lecompton Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lecompton_Constitution "Lecompton Constitution") and admission of Kansas as a [slave state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slave_state "Slave state").[\[134\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOates1974138%E2%80%93139-143) Many Illinois Republicans resented this eastern interference. For the first time, Illinois Republicans held a convention to agree upon a Senate candidate, and Lincoln won the nomination with little opposition.[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009247%E2%80%93250-144)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Lincoln_O-17_by_Brady%2C_1860.png/220px-Lincoln_O-17_by_Brady%2C_1860.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lincoln_O-17_by_Brady,_1860.png)

*Abraham Lincoln*, a portrait by [Mathew Brady](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mathew_Brady "Mathew Brady") taken February 27, 1860, the day of Lincoln's [Cooper Union speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooper_Union_speech "Cooper Union speech") in New York City

Lincoln accepted the nomination with great enthusiasm and zeal. After his nomination he delivered his [House Divided Speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln%27s_House_Divided_Speech "Lincoln's House Divided Speech"), with the biblical reference [Mark 3:25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_3#A_house_divided "Mark 3"), "A house divided against itself cannot stand. I believe this government cannot endure permanently half slave and half free. I do not expect the Union to be dissolved—I do not expect the house to fall—but I do expect it will cease to be divided. It will become all one thing, or all the other."[\[136\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009251-145) The speech created a stark image of the danger of disunion.[\[137\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarris200798-146) The stage was then set for the election of the Illinois legislature which would, in turn, select Lincoln or Douglas.[\[138\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996209-147) When informed of Lincoln's nomination, Douglas stated, "\[Lincoln] is the strong man of the party ... and if I beat him, my victory will be hardly won."[\[139\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009257%E2%80%93258-148)

The Senate campaign featured seven [debates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln%E2%80%93Douglas_debates "Lincoln–Douglas debates") between Lincoln and Douglas. These were the most famous political debates in American history; they had an atmosphere akin to a prizefight and drew crowds in the thousands.[\[140\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996214%E2%80%93218-149) The principals stood in stark contrast both physically and politically. Lincoln warned that the [Slave Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slave_Power "Slave Power") was threatening the values of republicanism, and he accused Douglas of distorting the Founding Fathers' premise that [all men are created equal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_men_are_created_equal "All men are created equal"). In his [Freeport Doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freeport_Doctrine "Freeport Doctrine"), Douglas argued that, despite the [Dred Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dred_Scott_v._Sandford "Dred Scott v. Sandford") decision, which he claimed to support,[\[141\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996202,_219,_232-150) local settlers, under the doctrine of [popular sovereignty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_sovereignty_in_the_United_States "Popular sovereignty in the United States"), should be free to choose whether to allow slavery within their territory, and he accused Lincoln of having joined the abolitionists.[\[142\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996214%E2%80%93224-151) Lincoln's argument assumed a moral tone, as he claimed that Douglas represented a conspiracy to promote slavery. Douglas's argument was more legal in nature, claiming that Lincoln was defying the authority of the U.S. Supreme Court as exercised in the *Dred Scott* decision.[\[143\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996223-152)

Though the Republican legislative candidates won more popular votes, the Democrats won more seats, and the legislature re-elected Douglas. However, Lincoln's articulation of the issues had given him a national political presence.[\[144\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTECarwardine200389%E2%80%9390-153) In May 1859, Lincoln purchased the *Illinois Staats-Anzeiger*, a German-language newspaper that was consistently supportive; most of the state's 130,000 German Americans voted for Democrats, but the German-language paper mobilized Republican support.[\[145\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996242,_412-154) In the aftermath of the 1858 election, newspapers frequently mentioned Lincoln as a potential Republican presidential candidate, rivaled by [William H. Seward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_H._Seward "William H. Seward"), [Salmon P. Chase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salmon_P._Chase "Salmon P. Chase"), [Edward Bates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Bates "Edward Bates"), and [Simon Cameron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Cameron "Simon Cameron"). While Lincoln was popular in the Midwest, he lacked support in the Northeast and was unsure whether to seek the office.[\[146\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009291%E2%80%93293-155) In January 1860, Lincoln told a group of political allies that he would accept the presidential nomination if offered and, in the following months, several local papers endorsed his candidacy.[\[147\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009307%E2%80%93308-156)

Over the coming months Lincoln was tireless, making nearly fifty speeches along the campaign trail. By the quality and simplicity of his rhetoric, he quickly became the champion of the Republican party. However, despite his overwhelming support in the [Midwestern United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States "Midwestern United States"), he was less appreciated in the east. [Horace Greeley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horace_Greeley "Horace Greeley"), editor of the New York Tribune, at that time wrote up an unflattering account of Lincoln's compromising position on slavery and his reluctance to challenge the court's *Dred Scott* ruling, which was promptly used against him by his political rivals.[\[148\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996200-157)[\[149\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorse1893112-158)

On February 27, 1860, powerful New York Republicans invited Lincoln to give a [speech at Cooper Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooper_Union_speech "Cooper Union speech"), in which he argued that the [Founding Fathers of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States "Founding Fathers of the United States") had little use for popular sovereignty and had repeatedly sought to restrict slavery. He insisted that morality required opposition to slavery and rejected any "groping for some middle ground between the right and the wrong".[\[150\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJaffa2000473-159) Many in the audience thought he appeared awkward and even ugly.[\[151\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolzer2004108%E2%80%93111-160) But Lincoln demonstrated intellectual leadership, which brought him into contention. Journalist [Noah Brooks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noah_Brooks "Noah Brooks") reported, "No man ever before made such an impression on his first appeal to a New York audience".[\[152\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTECarwardine200397Holzer2004157-161)

Historian [David Herbert Donald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Herbert_Donald "David Herbert Donald") described the speech as "a superb political move for an unannounced presidential aspirant. Appearing in Seward's home state, sponsored by a group largely loyal to Chase, Lincoln shrewdly made no reference to either of these Republican rivals for the nomination."[\[153\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996240-162) In response to an inquiry about his ambitions, Lincoln said, "The taste *is* in my mouth a little".[\[154\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996241-163)

### 1860 presidential election

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Abraham_Lincoln\&action=edit\&section=16 "Edit section: 1860 presidential election")]

Main article: [1860 United States presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1860_United_States_presidential_election "1860 United States presidential election")

[![Lincoln being carried by two men on a long board.](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/The_Rail_Candidate.jpg/220px-The_Rail_Candidate.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:The_Rail_Candidate.jpg)

*The Rail Candidate*, a critical [Currier and Ives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Currier_and_Ives "Currier and Ives") illustration, which depictied Lincoln's platform in the [1860 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1860_United_States_presidential_election "1860 United States presidential election") as being held up by a slave and his party

[![Map of the U.S. showing Lincoln winning the North-east and West, Breckinridge winning the South, Douglas winning Missouri, and Bell winning Virginia, West Virginia, and Kentucky.](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/ElectoralCollege1860.svg/220px-ElectoralCollege1860.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:ElectoralCollege1860.svg)

In the [1860 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1860_United_States_presidential_election "1860 United States presidential election"), northern and western [electoral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_College_\(United_States\) "Electoral College (United States)") votes (shown in red) put Lincoln into the [White House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House "White House").

On May 9–10, 1860, the Illinois Republican State Convention was held in [Decatur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decatur,_Illinois "Decatur, Illinois").[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996244-164) Lincoln's followers organized a campaign team led by [David Davis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Davis_\(Supreme_Court_justice\) "David Davis (Supreme Court justice)"), [Norman Judd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman_B._Judd "Norman B. Judd"), [Leonard Swett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonard_Swett "Leonard Swett"), and Jesse DuBois, and Lincoln received his first endorsement.[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOates1974175%E2%80%93176-165) Exploiting his embellished frontier legend (clearing land and splitting fence rails), Lincoln's supporters adopted the label of "The Rail Candidate".[\[157\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996245-166) In 1860, Lincoln described himself: "I am in height, six feet, four inches, nearly; lean in flesh, weighing, on an average, one hundred and eighty pounds; dark complexion, with coarse black hair, and gray eyes."[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-167) Michael Martinez wrote about the effective imaging of Lincoln by his campaign. At times he was presented as the plain-talking "Rail Splitter" and at other times he was "Honest Abe", unpolished but trustworthy.[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-168)

On May 18 at the [Republican National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1860_Republican_National_Convention "1860 Republican National Convention") in Chicago, Lincoln won the nomination on the third ballot, beating candidates such as Seward and Chase. A former Democrat, [Hannibal Hamlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannibal_Hamlin "Hannibal Hamlin") of Maine, was nominated for vice president to [balance the ticket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ticket_balance "Ticket balance"). Lincoln's success depended on his campaign team, his reputation as a moderate on the slavery issue, and his strong support for internal improvements and the tariff.[\[160\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELuthin1944609%E2%80%93629-169) Pennsylvania put him over the top, led by the state's iron interests who were reassured by his tariff support.[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHofstadter193850%E2%80%9355-170) Lincoln's managers had focused on this delegation while honoring Lincoln's dictate to "Make no contracts that will bind me".[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996247%E2%80%93250-171)

As the Slave Power tightened its grip on the national government, most Republicans agreed with Lincoln that the North was the aggrieved party. Throughout the 1850s, Lincoln had doubted the prospects of civil war, and his supporters rejected claims that his election would incite secession.[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBorittPinsker200210,_13,_18-172) When Douglas was selected as the candidate of the Northern Democrats, delegates from eleven slave states walked out of the [Democratic convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1860_Democratic_National_Convention "1860 Democratic National Convention"); they opposed Douglas's position on popular sovereignty, and selected incumbent Vice President [John C. Breckinridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_C._Breckinridge "John C. Breckinridge") as their candidate.[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996253-173) A group of former Whigs and Know Nothings formed the [Constitutional Union Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Union_Party_\(United_States\) "Constitutional Union Party (United States)") and nominated [John Bell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Bell_\(Tennessee_politician\) "John Bell (Tennessee politician)") of Tennessee. Lincoln and Douglas competed for votes in the North, while Bell and Breckinridge primarily found support in the South.[\[133\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009247%E2%80%93248-142)

Before the Republican convention, the Lincoln campaign began cultivating a nationwide youth organization, the [Wide Awakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wide_Awakes "Wide Awakes"), which it used to generate popular support throughout the country to spearhead voter registration drives, thinking that new voters and young voters tended to embrace new parties.[\[165\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-174) People of the Northern states knew the Southern states would vote against Lincoln and rallied supporters for Lincoln.[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-175)

As Douglas and the other candidates campaigned, Lincoln gave no speeches, relying on the enthusiasm of the Republican Party. The party did the leg work that produced majorities across the North and produced an abundance of campaign posters, leaflets, and newspaper editorials. Republican speakers focused first on the party platform, and second on Lincoln's life story, emphasizing his childhood poverty. The goal was to demonstrate the power of "free labor", which allowed a common farm boy to work his way to the top by his own efforts.[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996254%E2%80%93256-176) The Republican Party's production of campaign literature dwarfed the combined opposition; a *Chicago Tribune* writer produced a pamphlet that detailed Lincoln's life and sold 100,000–200,000 copies.[\[168\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996254-177) Though he did not give public appearances, many sought to visit him and write him. In the runup to the election, he took an office in the Illinois state capitol to deal with the influx of attention. He also hired [John George Nicolay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_George_Nicolay "John George Nicolay") as his personal secretary, who would remain in that role during the presidency.[\[169\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996251%E2%80%93252-178)

On November 6, 1860, Lincoln was elected the 16th president. He was the first Republican president and his victory was entirely due to his support in the North and West. No ballots were cast for him in 10 of the 15 Southern slave states, and he won only two of 996 counties in all the Southern states, an omen of the impending Civil War.[\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMansch200561-179)[\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDonald1996256-180) Lincoln received 1,866,452 votes, or 39.8% of the total in a four-way race, carrying the free Northern states, as well as California and Oregon.[\[172\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhite2009350-181) His victory in the [Electoral College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Electoral_College "United States Electoral College") was decisive: Lincoln had 180 votes to 123 for his opponents.[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTENevins19474:312-182)

## Presidency (1861–1865)
